=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is an infinitely large two-dimensional grid, with a campfire at coordinate (0,0).
At time t=0, smoke exists only at cell (0,0).
You are given a length-N string S consisting of N, W, S, E. At times t=1,2,\dots,N, the following happen in order:

- Wind blows, and all the smoke present at that time moves as follows:
- If the t-th character of S is N, smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c).
- If it is W, smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r,c-1).
- If it is S, smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r+1,c).
- If it is E, smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r,c+1).


- If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0).

Takahashi is standing at cell (R,C).
For each integer 1 \le t \le N, determine if smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5, and print the response according to the required format.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N R C
S

Output

Print an N-character string consisting of 0 and 1.
The t-th character (1 \le t \le N) should be:

- 1 if smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5, and
- 0 otherwise.

Constraints


- N is an integer between 1 and 200000, inclusive.
- S is a length N string consisting of N, W, S, E.
- R and C are integers between -N and N, inclusive.
- (R,C) \neq (0,0)

Sample Input 1

6 -2 1
NNEEWS

Sample Output 1

001010

At times 1.5,2.5,4.5,6.5, there is no smoke at cell (-2,1). At times 3.5,5.5, there is smoke at cell (-2,1).
Hence, output 001010.
In the figures below, taking cell (0,0) with the campfire as a reference, cell (r,c) is drawn:

- -r cells up if r < 0,
- r cells down if r \ge 0,
- -c cells left if c < 0,
- c cells right if c \ge 0.

The grid at time 0.5 looks like:

The grid at time 1.5 looks like:

The grid at time 2.5 looks like:

The grid at time 3.5 looks like:

The grid at time 4.5 looks like:

The grid at time 5.5 looks like:

The grid at time 6.5 looks like:

Sample Input 2

10 1 2
NEESESWEES

Sample Output 2

0001101011

Sample Input 3

20 -1 -2
WWNNWSWEWNSWWENSNWWN

Sample Output 3

00100111111000101111

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Option 1: Use tuple. Code:
import sys
def main():
    data=sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data: return
    N=int(data[0]); R=int(data[1]); C=int(data[2]); S=data[3].strip()
    first={(0,0):0}
    r=c=0
    ans=[]
    for t,ch in enumerate(S,1):
        if ch=='N': r-=1
        elif ch=='S': r+=1
        elif ch=='W': c-=1
        else: c+=1
        if (r,c) not in first:
            first[(r,c)] = t
        qr = r - R
        qc = c - C
        ft = first.get((qr,qc))
        if ft is not None and ft <= t:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')
    print(''.join(ans))
if __name__ == "__main__": main()

Now, code with set:
import sys
def main():
    input=sys.stdin.readline
    N,R,C=map(int,input().split())
    S=input().strip()
    visited={(0,0)}
    r=c=0
    ans=[]
    for ch in S:
        if ch=='N': r-=1
        elif ch=='S': r+=1
        elif ch=='W': c-=1
        else: c+=1
        visited.add((r,c))
        qr=r-R; qc=c-C
        ans.append('1' if (qr,qc) in visited else '0')
    print(''.join(ans))
if __name__=="__main__": main()

Let's maybe implement with integer encoding to be safe and avoid tuple overhead. But code readability? We can still write clear. Let's evaluate integer encoding with set.

Coordinate bounds: Prefix positions r,c ∈ [-N,N]. We can encode key = (r + N) * (2*N + 1) + (c + N). This maps [-N,N]^2 to [0,(2N+1)^2-1]. For q = pos - target, q components may be outside [-N,N]. If q outside, cannot be in visited because visited keys only within [-N,N]. But if we compute q_key using same formula with qr,qc maybe qr+N could be negative or >2N, qc+N could be negative or >2N; due to base, could collide with valid key as noted. Need handle bounds before encoding. We can check if -N <= qr <= N and -N <= qc <= N, then key = (qr+N)*BASE + (qc+N); else not in visited. But q components are ints. This check is cheap. However, q = P_t - target. P_t within [-N,N], target within [-N,N], so q within [-2N,2N]. It can be outside. Need bounds check.

Alternatively encode with offset 2N and base 4N+1? Since q within [-2N,2N], store prefixes within [-N,N] but key with offset 2N? If we use key=(r+2N)*(4N+1)+(c+2N) for all positions, then stored prefix keys are within [N,3N] etc. q keys within [0,4N]. No collision because base=4N+1 and components in range. But for stored prefixes, using offset 2N and base 4N+1 works and q within range. But we only add prefixes; q can be looked up with same encoding. This avoids bounds check. Memory similar. Let's see: N up to 200000, 4N+1 ~800001, offset 2N. Key max (4N)*(4N+1)+4N = (4N+1)^2 -1 ~ 6.4e11, fits Python int. Good. But using offset 2N means stored prefix (0,0) key = 2N*(4N+1)+2N = 2N*(4N+2)? Actually fine. q within [-2N,2N] because P_t and target both in [-N,N], so qr+2N ∈ [0,4N], qc+2N ∈ [0,4N]. No collision because base = 4N+1 and digits in [0,4N]. This is robust and avoids bounds check. But maybe overkill. Tuple simpler.

Could also encode as (r+OFFSET) << SHIFT | (c+OFFSET) with offset 2N and shift enough bits. But base multiplication fine.

Let's think if q can be outside [-2N,2N]? P_t coordinate after t steps: max absolute t <= N. target coordinate between -N,N. Difference min -2N max 2N. Yes. So offset 2N base 4N+1 covers all q. For stored prefixes, offset 2N gives digits from N to 3N? Wait if r=-N, r+2N=N; if r=N, 3N. All within 0..4N. Good. So we can use a single encoding for both visited and q without bounds check:
BASE = 4*N + 1
OFFSET = 2*N
def key(r,c): return (r+OFFSET)*BASE + (c+OFFSET)
visited = {key(0,0)}
...
visited.add(key(r,c))
qkey = key(r-R, c-C)  # q components within [-2N,2N]
ans.append('1' if qkey in visited else '0')

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    BASE = 4 * N + 1
    OFF = 2 * N

    visited = {OFF * BASE + OFF}  # (0,0)
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:  # 'E'
            c += 1

        visited.add((r + OFF) * BASE + (c + OFF))

        qr = r - R
        qc = c - C
        if (qr + OFF) * BASE + (qc + OFF) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:  # 'E'
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        qr = r - R
        qc = c - C
        if (qr, qc) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    print(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not int? Input ints. OK.

At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5" and if target is occupied by a particle with label v = P_t - target. Since v in visited. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5" and if target is occupied by a particle with label v = P_t - target. Since v in visited. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5" and if target is occupied by a particle with label v = P_t - target. Since v in visited. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with using `N` variable but not using it except input. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "visited.add((r,c))" before query and if q is a prefix position that appears at current time? no. But what if target = (0,0) excluded. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited.add((r, c))" before query and q could be a prefix position that is first visited at current time if target=0. Excluded. But if target nonzero, no. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5" and if target is occupied by a particle with label v = P_t - target. Since v in visited. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5" and if target is occupied by a particle with label v = P_t - target. Since v in visited. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with using `N` variable but not using it except input. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "visited.add((r,c))" before query and if q is a prefix position that appears at current time? no. But what if target = (0,0) excluded. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5" and if target is occupied by a particle with label v = P_t - target. Since v in visited. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5" and if target is occupied by a particle with label v = P_t - target. Since v in visited. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with using `N` variable but not using it except input. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "visited.add((r,c))" before query and if q is a prefix position that appears at current time? no. But what if target = (0,0) excluded. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not int? Input ints. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "data = sys.stdin.read().split()" and if S contains newline but no spaces, OK. If S is very long, split copies? It creates list of tokens; S token is a string of length N. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ans = []" and appending 200k chars, join. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "print(''.join(ans))" if ans empty? N>=1, not empty. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if ch == 'N': r -= 1" but if ch is 'N' and variable N maybe no. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "else: # 'E'" but if S contains unexpected char, would treat as E. Constraints guarantee. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited.add((r, c))" before query and q could be a prefix position that is first visited at current time if target=0. Excluded. But if target nonzero, no. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if ch == 'N': r -= 1" but if ch is 'N' and variable N maybe no. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited.add((r, c))" before query and q could be a prefix position that is first visited at current time if target=0. Excluded. But if target nonzero, no. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5" and if target is occupied by a particle with label v = P_t - target. Since v in visited. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with using `N` variable but not using it except input. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "visited.add((r,c))" before query and if q is a prefix position that appears at current time? no. But what if target = (0,0) excluded. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not int? Input ints. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "data = sys.stdin.read().split()" and if S contains newline but no spaces, OK. If S is very long, split copies? It creates list of tokens; S token is a string of length N. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ans = []" and appending 200k chars, join. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "print(''.join(ans))" if ans empty? N>=1, not empty. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if ch == 'N': r -= 1" but if ch is 'N' and variable N maybe no. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "else: # 'E'" but if S contains unexpected char, would treat as E. Constraints guarantee. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited.add((r, c))" before query and q could be a prefix position that is first visited at current time if target=0. Excluded. But if target nonzero, no. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if ch == 'N': r -= 1" but if ch is 'N' and variable N maybe no. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited.add((r, c))" before query and q could be a prefix position that is first visited at current time if target=0. Excluded. But if target nonzero, no. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5" and if target is occupied by a particle with label v = P_t - target. Since v in visited. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with using `N` variable but not using it except input. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "visited.add((r,c))" before query and if q is a prefix position that appears at current time? no. But what if target = (0,0) excluded. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not int? Input ints. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "data = sys.stdin.read().split()" and if S contains newline but no spaces, OK. If S is very long, split copies? It creates list of tokens; S token is a string of length N. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ans = []" and appending 200k chars, join. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "print(''.join(ans))" if ans empty? N>=1, not empty. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if ch == 'N': r -= 1" but if ch is 'N' and variable N maybe no. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "else: # 'E'" but if S contains unexpected char, would treat as E. Constraints guarantee. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited.add((r, c))" before query and q could be a prefix position that is first visited at current time if target=0. Excluded. But if target nonzero, no. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if ch == 'N': r -= 1" but if ch is 'N' and variable N maybe no. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited.add((r, c))" before query and q could be a prefix position that is first visited at current time if target=0. Excluded. But if target nonzero, no. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5" and if target is occupied by a particle with label v = P_t - target. Since v in visited. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with using `N` variable but not using it except input. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "visited.add((r,c))" before query and if q is a prefix position that appears at current time? no. But what if target = (0,0) excluded. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not int? Input ints. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "data = sys.stdin.read().split()" and if S contains newline but no spaces, OK. If S is very long, split copies? It creates list of tokens; S token is a string of length N. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ans = []" and appending 200k chars, join. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "print(''.join(ans))" if ans empty? N>=1, not empty. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if ch == 'N': r -= 1" but if ch is 'N' and variable N maybe no. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "else: # 'E'" but if S contains unexpected char, would treat as E. Constraints guarantee. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited.add((r, c))" before query and q could be a prefix position that is first visited at current time if target=0. Excluded. But if target nonzero, no. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if ch == 'N': r -= 1" but if ch is 'N' and variable N maybe no. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited.add((r, c))" before query and q could be a prefix position that is first visited at current time if target=0. Excluded. But if target nonzero, no. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5" and if target is occupied by a particle with label v = P_t - target. Since v in visited. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if ch == 'N': r -= 1" but if ch is 'N' and variable N maybe no. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited.add((r, c))" before query and q could be a prefix position that is first visited at current time if target=0. Excluded. But if target nonzero, no. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5" and if target is occupied by a particle with label v = P_t - target. Since v in visited. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with using `N` variable but not using it except input. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "visited.add((r,c))" before query and if q is a prefix position that appears at current time? no. But what if target = (0,0) excluded. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not int? Input ints. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "data = sys.stdin.read().split()" and if S contains newline but no spaces, OK. If S is very long, split copies? It creates list of tokens; S token is a string of length N. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ans = []" and appending 200k chars, join. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "print(''.join(ans))" if ans empty? N>=1, not empty. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if ch == 'N': r -= 1" but if ch is 'N' and variable N maybe no. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "else: # 'E'" but if S contains unexpected char, would treat as E. Constraints guarantee. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited.add((r, c))" before query and q could be a prefix position that is first visited at current time if target=0. Excluded. But if target nonzero, no. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if ch == 'N': r -= 1" but if ch is 'N' and variable N maybe no. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited.add((r, c))" before query and q could be a prefix position that is first visited at current time if target=0. Excluded. But if target nonzero, no. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5" and if target is occupied by a particle with label v = P_t - target. Since v in visited. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with using `N` variable but not using it except input. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "visited.add((r,c))" before query and if q is a prefix position that appears at current time? no. But what if target = (0,0) excluded. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not int? Input ints. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "data = sys.stdin.read().split()" and if S contains newline but no spaces, OK. If S is very long, split copies? It creates list of tokens; S token is a string of length N. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ans = []" and appending 200k chars, join. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "print(''.join(ans))" if ans empty? N>=1, not empty. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if ch == 'N': r -= 1" but if ch is 'N' and variable N maybe no. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "else: # 'E'" but if S contains unexpected char, would treat as E. Constraints guarantee. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited.add((r, c))" before query and q could be a prefix position that is first visited at current time if target=0. Excluded. But if target nonzero, no. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if ch == 'N': r -= 1" but if ch is 'N' and variable N maybe no. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited.add((r, c))" before query and q could be a prefix position that is first visited at current time if target=0. Excluded. But if target nonzero, no. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5" and if target is occupied by a particle with label v = P_t - target. Since v in visited. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with using `N` variable but not using it except input. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "visited.add((r,c))" before query and if q is a prefix position that appears at current time? no. But what if target = (0,0) excluded. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not int? Input ints. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "data = sys.stdin.read().split()" and if S contains newline but no spaces, OK. If S is very long, split copies? It creates list of tokens; S token is a string of length N. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ans = []" and appending 200k chars, join. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "print(''.join(ans))" if ans empty? N>=1, not empty. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if ch == 'N': r -= 1" but if ch is 'N' and variable N maybe no. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "else: # 'E'" but if S contains unexpected char, would treat as E. Constraints guarantee. OK.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited.add((r, c))" before query and q could be a prefix position that is first visited at current time if target=0. Excluded. But if target nonzero, no. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "P_t - target" if target is (R,C) and P_t is current prefix. We compute qr = r - R, qc = c - C. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "smoke in cell (r,c) moves to cell (r-1,c)" for N, so prefix displacement after N wind is (-1,0). We do. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "At times t=1,2,...,N, the following happen in order" and "If there is no smoke in cell (0,0), new smoke is generated at cell (0,0)." At time t, after wind, if origin empty, generate. We model generation at time t if P_t not in visited. But what if origin empty and P_t is in visited? We proved impossible. Let's prove with labels: If P_t in visited, there is a particle with label P_t. At time t after wind, that particle is at P_t - P_t = origin. So origin occupied. Thus no generation. If P_t not in visited, no particle with label P_t, and since positions are P_t - v, origin occupied iff v=P_t. So origin empty. Thus generation iff P_t not in visited. Good. This is the key invariant. Nice.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "visited" set containing labels of particles that exist at time t+0.5. We add P_t if new. If P_t duplicate, no new label. Good.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "smoke exists at cell (R,C) at time t+0.5" and if target is occupied by a particle with label v = P_t - target. Since v in visited. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R,C" maybe not within prefix range but q can be outside visited. OK.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "N" length and "S" string maybe includes carriage return \r? `split()` strips. OK.

Now, let's decide final code. I will output tuple set code. Let's maybe include `sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans) + '\n')` to ensure newline. `print` is fine. Use `sys.stdout.write` for speed. Let's write:

```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    R = int(data[1])
    C = int(data[2])
    S = data[3]

    visited = {(0, 0)}
    r = 0
    c = 0
    ans = []

    for ch in S:
        if ch == 'N':
            r -= 1
        elif ch == 'S':
            r += 1
        elif ch == 'W':
            c -= 1
        else:
            c += 1

        visited.add((r, c))

        if (r - R, c - C) in visited:
            ans.append('1')
        else:
            ans.append('0')

    sys.stdout.write(''.join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

This is concise. Note: We compute `(r - R, c - C)` after adding current pos. If target nonzero, no false positive. Good.
